Re-measured by rebuilding `6e22718` in a throwaway worktree: baseline **1.828 s**, C2 **1.829 s**, six runs each — under 0.1 %. The 1.782 s reading was an outlier. Expected structurally too: the loop is line-for-line the old one bar renames, and C2 *removes* work (a three-state compare becomes a bool; the per-region restart is gone).
- **✅ C3 done and reviewed — and `filtering.rs` now holds exactly what the design says it should** — [impl](doc/devel/reports/implementations/ng_read_filtering_stages_c3_2026-08-03.md), [review](doc/devel/reports/reviews/ng_read_filtering_stages_c3_2026-08-03.md) (**2 Blocker / 2 Major / 1 Minor**), [fixes](doc/devel/reports/reviews/fixes_applied_2026-08-03_v9.md). `RecordSource` and both doubles deleted; `RegionRawAlignedReads`'s four methods are inherent. **The checkpoint condition is met**: the file's whole top-level surface is `ReadFilterConfig`, `FilterVerdict`, `DropReason`, `ReadFilterCounts` and the two verdicts — spec §6's list exactly, nothing more. **1,787 → 781 lines** across the milestone. Suite **2,858**.
- **⚠ TWO BLOCKERS, BOTH IN LINES C3 MOVED WITHOUT CHANGING — and the step's own safety argument is why they were worth hunting.** C3 argued "the bodies are byte-identical, so the tests still cover them", which is right in principle and exactly why the reviewer mutated instead of trusting it: identical bodies stay as covered as they were, and these two were not covered at all. **(1)** The held record's read-group replay: `None` survives all 2,856 and kills an otherwise valid multi-read-group CRAM run at every region boundary (such a record carries no `RG` tag to fall back on); `Some(ReadGroupId(0))` also survives and **silently attributes a read to the wrong library**, destroying the per-read-group signal spec §7 exists to protect. **(2)** The early stop's `on_this_contig &&` guard: dropping it survives all 2,856, and because a sorted file groups contigs, a record of a *later* contig then ends the walk and loses every remaining read of the region. The existing fixture could not see it — its foreign-contig record sits *inside* the region, so the position test never fires on it. Both now killed by their own tests, alone.
- **⚠ C3's report claimed `FakeRecord::decode_fails` was "the only construction of `ReadFilterError::Decode`". False twice.** `FakeRecord` never constructed a `ReadFilterError` at all — it returned an `io::Error` the deleted filter wrapped — and by C3 `decode_fails: true` appeared **nowhere**: both constructors set `false`, the two tests that set it having died at **C2**. So C3 deleted dead code. `Decode` did become unreachable *and* unpinned, at **C2**; the record now lives **on the variant itself**, since C1's note was inside the test block C3 deleted, and its doc no longer names a cause ("the unmapped flag clear yet no position") that the layer below discards first.
- **⚦ A C2 defect found by C3's reviewer: `AlignmentCursor`'s doc comment was severed.** C2's insertion of the error and tally types landed *inside* it, so a 27-line block describing the cursor was attached to `ReadFilterError` and the cursor's own rustdoc was the orphan fragment *"is no stream object to give back."* Repaired. `cargo doc` reached 13 three times across this milestone and the 12-link baseline caught every one.
- **⛦ Two arch §3.3 discrepancies, both resolved in the code's favour and left for the checkpoint.** §3.3 lists a fifth inherent method, `header()` — but `RecordSource::header` went at B1 with the contig probe that was its only caller, and arch §6 already says "re-add it at C3 only if a caller appears". None has (grep-verified), so it is not re-added. §3.3 also spells `other_sample_reads` where the code says `other_sample_records`; the code is right — the layer counts *records* it skipped, before anything became a read.
